===PAGE 173=== (plain, 41 lines)
   1| 173
   2| ARTICLE 20. To see if the Town will vote to amend the General Bylaws, by adding the following text as Chapter 204
   3| Sections 1 and 2; or take any action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board) Board of Selectmen recommends
   4| approval. Finance Committee no direct impact.
   5| 204-1. STORMWATER MANAGEMENT – NPDES PHASE II PERMITS
   6| A. Purpose and intent.
   7| (1) Regulation of discharges to the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) is necessary for the protection of the
   8| Town of Lunenburg's water bodies and groundwater, and to safeguard the public health, safety, welfare and the
   9| environment. Increased and contaminated stormwater runoff associated with developed land uses and the
  10| accompanying increase in impervious surface are major causes of impairment of water quality and flow in lakes,
  11| ponds, streams, rivers, wetlands, and groundwater which result in the contamination of drinking water supplies;
  12| erosion of stream channels; alteration or destruction of aquatic and wildlife habitat; and flooding. This bylaw
  13| establishes minimum stormwater management standards for the final conditions that result from development and
  14| redevelopment projects to minimize adverse impacts off-site and downstream which would be borne by abutters,
  15| townspeople and the general public.
  16| (2) This regulation requires local review and approval of a stormwater management plan for all development and
  17| redevelopment projects that disturb one acre or more. In addition to these regulations, the owner and/or developer is
  18| also obligated to meet the requirements of the Federal Environmental Protection Agency's (EPA) regulations for
  19| stormwater management.
  20| B. The objectives of this bylaw are:
  21| (1) To require practices to control the flow of stormwater from new and redeveloped sites to the Town's storm drainage
  22| system in order to prevent flooding and erosion;
  23| (2) To protect groundwater and surface water from degradation;
  24| (3) To promote groundwater recharge;
  25| (4) To prevent pollutants from entering the Town's municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4) and to minimize
  26| discharge of pollutants from the MS4;
  27| (5) To ensure adequate long-term operation and maintenance of structural stormwater best management practices so that
  28| they work as designed;
  29| (6) To comply with state and federal statutes and regulations relating to stormwater discharges; and
  30| (7) To establish Lunenburg's legal authority to ensure compliance with the provisions of this bylaw through inspection,
  31| monitoring, and enforcement.
  32| C. Applicability. No person may undertake a construction activity following the effective date of this Bylaw, including
  33| clearing, grading and excavation that results in a land disturbance that will disturb equal to or greater than one acre
  34| of land or will disturb less than one acre of land but part of a larger common plan of development or sale that will
  35| ultimately disturb equal to or greater than one acre of land draining to the Town of Lunenburg's municipal separate
  36| storm sewer system, without a permit from the special permit granting authority. Construction activity does not
  37| include routine maintenance that is performed to maintain the original line and grade, hydraulic capacity or the
  38| original purpose of the site. Construction activities that are exempt are:
  39| (1) Normal maintenance and improvement of land in agricultural use as defined by the Wetlands Protection Act
  40| regulation, 310 CMR 10.04;
  41| (2) Maintenance of existing landscaping, gardens or lawn areas associated with a single-family dwelling;

===PAGE 174=== (plain, 41 lines)
   1| 174
   2| (3) The construction of fencing that will not substantially alter existing terrain or drainage patterns;
   3| (4) Construction of utilities other than drainage (gas, water, electric, telephone, etc.) which will not alter terrain or
   4| drainage patterns;
   5| (5) Work activities of municipal, state or federal agencies or their agents outside of the Phase II boundaries as shown on
   6| the latest U.S. Environmental Protection Agency's National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System (NPDES) Phase
   7| II maps.
   8| (6) As authorized in the Phase II Small MS4 General Permit for Massachusetts, stormwater discharges resulting from
   9| the activities identified in Subsection C(1) that are wholly subject to jurisdiction under the Wetlands Protection Act
  10| and demonstrate compliance with the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Policy as reflected in an order of
  11| conditions issued by the Conservation Commission are exempt from compliance with this bylaw.
  12| D. Permits and procedures.
  13| (1) The special permit granting authority (SPGA) under this bylaw shall be the Lunenburg Planning Board. Such special
  14| permit shall be granted if the SPGA determines, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission, Department of
  15| Public Works, Board of Health and Building Commissioner, that the intent of this bylaw, as well as specific criteria,
  16| are met. The SPGA shall not grant a special permit under this section unless the petitioner's application materials
  17| include, in the SPGA's opinion, sufficiently detailed, definite, and credible information to support positive findings
  18| in relation to the standards given in this section. The SPGA shall document the basis for any departures from the
  19| recommendations of the other Town boards or departments in its decision.
  20| (2) The site owner or his agent shall file with the SPGA 10 copies of a completed application package for a stormwater
  21| management permit (SMP). Permit issuance is required prior to any site-altering activity. While the applicant can be
  22| a representative, the permittee must be the owner of the site. The SMP application package shall include:
  23| (a) A completed application form with original signatures of all owners;
  24| (b) Ten copies of the stormwater management plan and project description as specified in Subsection E(1);
  25| (c) Ten copies of the operation and maintenance plan as required by Subsection F of this bylaw;
  26| (d) Payment of the application and review fees.
  27| (3) Entry. Filing and application for a permit grants the SPGA, or its agent, permission to enter the site to verify the
  28| information in the application and to inspect for compliance with the resulting permit.
  29| (4) Other boards. The SPGA shall give one copy of the application package to the designated technical reviewers, which
  30| include the Conservation Commission, Department of Public Works, and Building Commissioner, for the purpose of
  31| reviewing the permit application. The SPGA may also engage the services of a peer review engineer at its discretion.
  32| (5) Fee structure. The SPGA shall obtain with each submission an application fee established by the SPGA. The SPGA
  33| is authorized to retain a registered professional engineer or other professional consultant to advise the SPGA on any
  34| or all aspects of these plans. Applicants must pay review fees before the review process may begin.
  35| (6) Actions. The SPGA's action, rendered in writing, shall consist of either:
  36| (a) Approval of the stormwater management permit application based upon determination that the proposed plan meets
  37| the standards in Subsection E(2) and will adequately protect the water resources of the community and is in
  38| compliance with the requirements set forth in this bylaw;
  39| (b) Approval of the stormwater management permit application subject to any conditions, modifications or restrictions
  40| required by the SPGA which will ensure that the project meets the standards in Subsection E(2) and adequately
  41| protects water resources, as set forth in this bylaw;

===PAGE 175=== (plain, 35 lines)
   1| 175
   2| (c) Disapproval of the stormwater management permit application based upon a determination that the proposed plan, as
   3| submitted, does not meet the standards in Subsection E(2) or adequately protect water resources, as set forth in this
   4| bylaw.
   5| (7) Project completion. At completion of the project, the permittee shall submit as-built record drawings of all structural
   6| stormwater controls and treatment best management practices required for the site. The as-built drawing shall show
   7| deviations from the approved plans, if any, and be certified by a registered professional engineer.
   8| E. Contents of stormwater management plan.
   9| (1) Application. The stormwater management plan shall contain sufficient information for the SPGA to evaluate the
  10| environmental impact, effectiveness, and acceptability of the measures proposed by the applicant for reducing
  11| adverse impacts from stormwater. The plan shall be designed to meet the Massachusetts stormwater management
  12| standards as set forth in Subsection E(2) below and the current edition of the Department of Environmental
  13| Protection guidelines and policies. The plan shall be designed to also meet the policy standards of the SPGA. The
  14| stormwater management plan shall fully describe the project in drawings, and narrative. It shall include:
  15| (a) Locus map;
  16| (b) The existing zoning, and land use at the site;
  17| (c) The proposed land use;
  18| (d) The location(s) of existing and proposed easements;
  19| (e) The location of existing and proposed utilities;
  20| (f) The site's existing and proposed topography, with contours at two-foot intervals;
  21| (g) The existing site hydrology;
  22| (h) A description and delineation of existing stormwater conveyances, impoundments, and wetlands on or adjacent to
  23| the site or into which stormwater flows;
  24| (i) A delineation of one-hundred-year floodplains, if applicable;
  25| (j) Estimated high groundwater elevation in areas to be used for stormwater retention, detention, or infiltration;
  26| (k) The existing and proposed vegetation and ground surfaces, with runoff coefficient for each;
  27| (l) A drainage area map showing pre- and post-construction watershed boundaries, drainage area and stormwater flow
  28| paths;
  29| (m) A description and drawings of all components of the proposed drainage system, including:
  30| [1] Locations, cross sections, and profiles of all brooks, streams, drainage swales and their method of stabilization,;
  31| [2] All measures for the detention, retention, or infiltration of water;
  32| [3] All measures for the protection of water quality;
  33| [4] The structural details for all components of the proposed drainage systems and stormwater management facilities;
  34| [5] Notes on drawings specifying materials to be used, construction specifications, and typicals; and
  35| [6] Expected hydrology, with supporting calculations;

===PAGE 176=== (plain, 39 lines)
   1| 176
   2| (n) Proposed improvements, including location of buildings or other structures, impervious surfaces, and drainage
   3| facilities, if applicable;
   4| (o) Timing, schedules, and sequence of development, including clearing, stripping, rough grading, construction, final
   5| grading, and vegetative stabilization;
   6| (p) A maintenance schedule for the period of construction; and
   7| (q) Any other information requested by the SPGA.
   8| (2) Standards. Projects shall meet the standards of the Massachusetts Stormwater Management Policy, which are as
   9| follows:
  10| (a) No new stormwater conveyances (e.g., outfalls) may discharge untreated stormwater directly to or cause erosion in
  11| wetlands or water of the commonwealth.
  12| (b) Stormwater management systems must be designed so that post-development peak discharges rates do not exceed
  13| pre-development peak discharge rates.
  14| (c) Loss of annual recharge to groundwater should be minimized through the use of infiltration measures to the
  15| maximum extent practicable. The annual recharge from the post-development site should approximate the annual
  16| recharge rate from the pre-development or existing site conditions, based on soil types.
  17| (d) For new development, stormwater management systems must be designed to remove 80% of the average annual load
  18| (post-development conditions) of total suspended solids (TSS). It is presumed that this standard is met when:
  19| [1] Suitable nonstructural practices for source control and pollution prevention are implemented;
  20| [2] Stormwater management best management practices (BMPs) are sized to capture the prescribed runoff volume; and
  21| [3] Stormwater management BMPs are maintained as designed.
  22| (e) Stormwater discharges from areas with higher potential pollutant loads require the use of specific stormwater
  23| management BMPs. The use of infiltration practices without pretreatment is prohibited.
  24| (f) Stormwater discharges to critical areas must utilize certain stormwater management BMPs approved for critical
  25| areas (see MA DEP's Stormwater Management Volume I: Stormwater Policy Handbook). Critical areas are
  26| outstanding resource waters (ORWs), cold-water fisheries, and recharge areas for public water supplies.
  27| (g) Redevelopment of previously developed sites must meet the stormwater management standards to the maximum
  28| extent practicable. However, if it is not practicable to meet all the standards, new (retrofitted or expanded)
  29| stormwater management systems must be designed to improve existing conditions.
  30| (h) Erosion and sediment controls must be implemented to prevent impacts during disturbance and construction
  31| activities.
  32| (i) All stormwater management systems must have an operation and maintenance plan to ensure that systems function
  33| as designed.
  34| (j) When one or more of the standards cannot be met, an applicant may demonstrate that an equivalent level of
  35| environmental protection will be provided.
  36| (3) Project changes. The permittee, or its agent, shall notify the SPGA in writing of any change or alteration of a
  37| land-disturbing activity authorized in a stormwater management permit before any change or alteration occurs. If the
  38| SPGA determines that the change or alteration is significant, based on the design standards listed in Subsection E(2) and
  39| accepted construction practices, the SPGA may require that an amended stormwater management permit

===PAGE 177=== (plain, 37 lines)
   1| 177
   2| application be filed and a public hearing held. If any change or deviation from the stormwater management permit occurs
   3| during a project, the SPGA may require the installation of interim measures before approving the change.
   4| F. Operation and maintenance plans. An operation and maintenance plan (O&M plan) is required at the time of
   5| application for all projects. The maintenance plan shall be designed to ensure compliance with the permit, this bylaw
   6| and that the Massachusetts Surface Water Quality Standards, 314 CMR 4.00 are met in all seasons and throughout
   7| the life of the system. The operation and maintenance plan shall remain on file with the SPGA and shall be an
   8| ongoing requirement. The O&M plan shall include:
   9| (1) The name(s) of the owner(s) for all components of the system.
  10| (2) Maintenance agreements that specify:
  11| (a) The names and addresses of the person(s) responsible for operation and maintenance.
  12| (b) The person(s) responsible for financing maintenance and emergency repairs.
  13| (3) Maintenance schedule for all drainage structures, including swales and ponds.
  14| (4) List of easements, with the purpose and location of each.
  15| (5) The signature(s) of the owner(s).
  16| (6) Stormwater management easement(s).
  17| (a) Stormwater management easements shall be provided by the property owner(s) as necessary for:
  18| [1] Access for facility inspections and maintenance.
  19| [2] Preservation of stormwater runoff conveyance, infiltration, and detention areas and facilities, including flood routes
  20| for the one-hundred-year storm event.
  21| [3] Direct maintenance access by heavy equipment to structures requiring regular cleanout.
  22| (b) The purpose of each easement shall be specified in the maintenance agreement signed by the property owner.
  23| (c) Stormwater management easements are required for all areas used for off-site stormwater control, unless a waiver is
  24| granted by the SPGA.
  25| (d) Easements shall be recorded with the Worcester County Registry of Deeds prior to issuance of a certificate of
  26| completion by the SPGA.
  27| (7) Changes to operation and maintenance plans.
  28| (a) The owner(s) of the stormwater management system must notify the SPGA of changes in ownership or assignment
  29| of financial responsibility.
  30| (b) The maintenance schedule in the maintenance agreement may be amended to achieve the purposes of this bylaw by
  31| mutual agreement of the SPGA and the responsible parties. Amendments must be in writing and signed by all
  32| responsible parties. Responsible parties shall include the owner(s), persons with financial responsibility, and persons
  33| with operational responsibility.
  34| G. Surety. The SPGA may require the permittee to post, before the start of land disturbance or construction activity,
  35| a surety bond, cash, or other acceptable security. The form of the bond shall be approved by Town Counsel, and be in an
  36| amount deemed sufficient by the SPGA to ensure that the work will be completed in accordance with the permit. If the
  37| project is phased, the SPGA may release part of the bond as each phase is completed in compliance with the

===PAGE 178=== (plain, 39 lines)
   1| 178
   2| permit, but the bond may not be fully released until the SPGA has received the final inspection report as required by
   3| Subsection H and issued a certificate of completion.
   4| H. Inspections.
   5| (1) At the discretion of the SPGA, periodic inspections of the stormwater management system construction may be
   6| conducted by the Town via the Planning Board, Conservation Commission, DPW or a professional engineer
   7| approved by the SPGA. Written reports shall include:
   8| (a) Inspection date and location.
   9| (b) Evaluation of compliance with the stormwater permit.
  10| (c) Any variations from approved specifications or any violations of the stormwater management plan.
  11| (2) At a minimum, the SPGA or its designee may inspect the project site at the following stages:
  12| (a) Initial site inspection: prior to approval of any plan.
  13| (b) Erosion control inspection: to ensure erosion control practices are in accord with the filed plan.
  14| (c) Bury inspection: prior to backfilling of any underground drainage stormwater conveyance structures.
  15| (d) Final inspection. After the stormwater management system has been constructed and before the surety has been
  16| released, the applicant must submit a record plan detailing the actual stormwater management system as installed.
  17| The SPGA or its agent may inspect the system to confirm its "as-built" features. This inspector may also evaluate the
  18| effectiveness of the system in an actual storm. If the inspector finds the system to be adequate he shall so report to
  19| the SPGA, which will issue a certificate of completion. As-built plans shall be full-size plans that include all final
  20| grades, prepared by a professional engineer. All changes to project design should be clearly depicted on the as-built
  21| plans.
  22| (e) If the system is found to be inadequate by virtue of physical evidence of operational failure, even though it was built
  23| as called for in the stormwater management plan, it shall be corrected by the permittee before the performance
  24| guarantee is released. If the permittee fails to act, the Town of Lunenburg may use the surety bond to complete the
  25| work. If the system does not comply with the plan, the permittee shall be notified in writing of the violation and the
  26| required corrective actions. A stop-work order shall be issued until any violations are corrected and all work
  27| previously completed has received approval by the SPGA.
  28| I. Waivers.
  29| (1) The SPGA may waive strict compliance with any requirement of this bylaw or the rules and regulations promulgated
  30| hereunder, where such action:
  31| (a) Is allowed by federal, state and local statutes and/or regulations;
  32| (b) Is in the public interest; and
  33| (c) Is not inconsistent with the purpose and intent of this bylaw.
  34| (2) Any applicant may submit a written request to be granted such a waiver. Such a request shall be accompanied by an
  35| explanation or documentation supporting the waiver request and demonstrating that strict application of the bylaws
  36| does not further the purposes or objectives of this bylaw.
  37| (3) All waiver requests shall be discussed and voted on at the public hearing for the project.
  38| (4) If, in the SPGA's opinion, additional time or information is required for review of a waiver request, the SPGA
  39| may

===PAGE 179=== (plain, 43 lines)
   1| 179
   2| continue the hearing to a date certain announced at the meeting. In the event the applicant objects to a continuance, or fails
   3| to provide requested information, the waiver request shall be denied.
   4| J. Certificate of completion. The SPGA will issue a letter certifying completion upon receipt and approval of the final
   5| inspection reports and/or upon otherwise determining that all work of the permit has been satisfactorily completed in
   6| conformance with this bylaw.
   7| K. Enforcement.
   8| (1) Enforcement agents. The Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation
   9| Commission and DPW Director, shall enforce this bylaw, regulations, orders, violation notices, and enforcement
  10| orders, and may pursue all civil and criminal remedies for such violations.
  11| L. Civil relief. If a person violates the provisions of this bylaw, regulations, a permit, notice, or order issued thereunder,
  12| the Planning Board, through the Building Commissioner, may seek injunctive relief in a court of competent
  13| jurisdiction restraining the person from activities which would create further violations or compelling the person to
  14| perform abatement or remediation of the violation.
  15| M. Orders.
  16| (1) The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and DPW Director, may issue a written
  17| order to the Building Commissioner to enforce the provisions of this bylaw or the regulations thereunder, which may
  18| include:
  19| (a) Elimination of illicit connections or discharges to the MS4;
  20| (b) Performance of monitoring, analyses, and reporting;
  21| (c) That unlawful discharges, practices, or operations shall cease and desist;
  22| (d) Remediation of contamination in connection therewith.
  23| (2) If the Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW
  24| Director, determines that abatement or remediation of contamination is required, the order shall set forth a deadline
  25| by which such abatement or remediation must be completed. Said order shall further advise that, should the violator
  26| or property owner fail to abate or perform remediation within the specified deadline, the Town may, at its option,
  27| undertake such work, and expenses thereof shall be charged to the violator.
  28| (3) Within 30 days after completing all measures necessary to abate the violation or to perform remediation, the violator
  29| and the property owner will be notified of the cost incurred by the Town, including administrative costs. The violator
  30| or property owner may file a written protest objecting to the amount or basis of costs with the Planning Board within
  31| 30 days of receipt of the notification of the cost incurred. If the amount due is not received by the expiration of the
  32| time in which to file a protest or within 30 days following a decision of the Planning Board affirming or reducing the
  33| costs, or from a final decision of a court of competent jurisdiction, the costs shall become a special assessment
  34| against the property owner and shall constitute a lien on the owner's property for the amount of said costs. Interest
  35| shall begin to accrue on any unpaid costs at the statutory rate provided in MGL c. 59, § 57 after the 31st day at
  36| which the costs first become due.
  37| N. Noncriminal disposition; right of entry.
  38| (1) As an alternative to criminal prosecution or civil action, the Planning Board may elect, through the Building
  39| Commissioner, to utilize the noncriminal disposition procedure set forth in MGL c. 40, § 21D, and Chapter 1, Article I, of
  40| the General Bylaws of the Town, in which case the Planning Board, its authorized agents and the Building Commissioner
  41| of the Town shall be the enforcing person. The penalty for the first violation shall be $25. The penalty for the second
  42| violation shall be $50. The penalty for the third violation shall be $100 and $200 for the fourth and each subsequent
  43| violation. Each day or part thereof that such violation occurs or continues shall constitute a separate

===PAGE 180=== (plain, 39 lines)
   1| 180
   2| offense.
   3| (2) Entry to perform duties under this bylaw. To the extent permitted by state law, or if authorized by the owner or other
   4| party in control of the property, the Planning Board, its agents, and employees may enter upon privately owned
   5| property for the purpose of performing their duties under this bylaw and regulations and may make or cause to be
   6| made such examination surveys or sampling as the Planning Board deems reasonably necessary.
   7| O. Appeals. The decisions or orders of the Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and
   8| DPW, shall be final. Further relief shall be to a court of competent jurisdiction.
   9| P. Remedies not exclusive. The remedies listed in this bylaw are not exclusive of any other remedies available under
  10| any applicable federal, state or local law.
  11| Q. Severability. The invalidity of any section, provision, paragraph, sentence, or clause of these regulations shall not
  12| invalidate any section, provision, paragraph, sentence, or clause thereof, nor shall it invalidate any permit or
  13| determination that previously has been issued.
  14| 204-2 Discharges to municipal storm sewer system and waters of commonwealth; NPDES Phase II
  15| permits.
  16| A. Purpose.
  17| (1) Increased and contaminated stormwater runoff is a major cause of impairment of water quality and flow in lakes,
  18| ponds, streams, rivers, wetlands and groundwater; contamination of drinking water supplies; alteration or destruction
  19| of aquatic and wildlife habitat; flooding.
  20| (2) Regulation of illicit connections and discharges to the municipal storm sewer system is necessary for the protection
  21| of the Town's water bodies and groundwater, and to safeguard the public health, safety, welfare and the
  22| environment.
  23| (3) The objectives of this bylaw are:
  24| (a) To prevent pollutants from entering the Town's municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4);
  25| (b) To prohibit illicit connections and unauthorized discharges to the MS4;
  26| (c) To require the removal of all such illicit connections;
  27| (d) To comply with state and federal statutes and regulations relating to stormwater discharges; and
  28| (e) To establish the legal authority to ensure compliance with the provisions of this bylaw through inspection,
  29| monitoring, and enforcement.
  30| B. Definitions. For the purposes of this bylaw, the following shall mean:
  31| AUTHORIZED ENFORCEMENT AGENCY
  32| The Planning Board (hereafter the "Board"), its employees or agents designated to administer/enforce this bylaw.
  33| BEST MANAGEMENT PRACTICE (BMP)
  34| An activity, procedure, restraint, or structural improvement that helps to reduce the quantity or improve the quality
  35| of stormwater runoff.
  36| CLEAN WATER ACT
  37| The Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 U.S.C. § 1251 et seq.) as hereafter amended.
  38| DISCHARGE OF POLLUTANTS
  39| The addition from any source of any pollutant or combination of pollutants into the municipal storm sewer system or

===PAGE 181=== (plain, 41 lines)
   1| 181
   2| into the waters of the United States or commonwealth from any source.
   3| GROUNDWATER
   4| Water beneath the surface of the ground.
   5| ILLICIT CONNECTION
   6| A surface or subsurface sewer or conveyance, which allows an illicit discharge into the municipal storm sewer
   7| system, including without limitation sewage, process wastewater, or wash water and any connections from indoor
   8| drains, sinks, or toilets, regardless of whether said connection was previously allowed, permitted, or approved before
   9| the effective date of this bylaw.
  10| ILLICIT DISCHARGE
  11| Direct or indirect discharge to the municipal storm sewer system that is not composed entirely of stormwater, except
  12| as exempted in Subsection H. The term does not include a discharge in compliance with an NPDES stormwater
  13| discharge permit or a surface water discharge permit, or resulting from fire-fighting activities exempted pursuant to
  14| Subsection H of this bylaw.
  15| IMPERVIOUS SURFACE
  16| Any material or structure on or above the ground that prevents water infiltrating the underlying soil. Impervious
  17| surface includes without limitation roads, paved parking lots, sidewalks, and rooftops.
  18| MUNICIPAL SEPARATE STORM DRAIN SYSTEM (MS4) or MUNICIPAL STORM SEWER SYSTEM
  19| The system of conveyances designed or used for collecting or conveying stormwater, including any road with a
  20| drainage system, street, gutter, curb, inlet, piped storm drain, pumping facility, retention or detention basin, natural
  21| or man-made or altered drainage channel, reservoir, and other drainage structure that together comprise the storm
  22| drainage system owned or operated by the Town of Lunenburg.
  23| NATIONAL POLLUTANT DISCHARGE ELIMINATION SYSTEM (NPDES) STORMWATER DISCHARGE
  24| PERMIT
  25| A permit issued by United States Environmental Protection Agency or jointly with the state that authorizes the
  26| discharge of pollutants to waters of the United States.
  27| NON-STORMWATER DISCHARGE
  28| Discharge to the municipal storm sewer system not composed entirely of stormwater.
  29| PERSON
  30| An individual, partnership, association, firm, company, trust, corporation, agency, authority, department or political
  31| subdivision of the commonwealth or the federal government, to the extent permitted by law, and any officer,
  32| employee, or agent of such person.
  33| POLLUTANT
  34| Any element or property of sewage, agricultural, industrial or commercial waste, runoff, leachate, heated effluent, or
  35| other matter, whether originating at a point or nonpoint source, that is or may be introduced into any sewage
  36| treatment works or waters of the commonwealth. Pollutants may include but are not limited to:
  37| (1) Paints, varnishes, and solvents;
  38| (2) Oil and other automotive fluids;
  39| (3) Nonhazardous liquid and solid wastes and yard wastes;
  40| (4) Refuse, rubbish, garbage, litter, or other discarded or abandoned objects, ordnances, accumulations and floatables;
  41| (5) Pesticides, herbicides, and fertilizers;

===PAGE 182=== (plain, 40 lines)
   1| 182
   2| (6) Hazardous materials and wastes; sewage, fecal coliform and pathogens;
   3| (7) Dissolved and particulate metals;
   4| (8) Animal wastes;
   5| (9) Rock, sand, salt, soils;
   6| (10) Construction wastes and residues;
   7| (11) Noxious or offensive matter of any kind.
   8| PROCESS WASTEWATER
   9| Water which during manufacturing or processing comes into direct contact with or results from the production or use
  10| of any material, intermediate product, finished product, or waste product.
  11| RECHARGE
  12| The process by which groundwater is replenished by precipitation through the percolation of runoff and surface
  13| water through the soil.
  14| STORMWATER
  15| Stormwater runoff, snow melt runoff, and surface water runoff and drainage.
  16| SURFACE WATER DISCHARGE PERMIT
  17| A permit issued by the Department of Environmental Protection (DEP) pursuant to 314 CMR 3.00 that authorizes
  18| the discharge of pollutants to waters of the Commonwealth of Massachusetts.
  19| TOXIC OR HAZARDOUS MATERIAL OR WASTE
  20| Any material which, because of its quantity, concentration, chemical, corrosive, flammable, reactive, toxic,
  21| infectious or radioactive characteristics, either separately or in combination with any substance or substances,
  22| constitutes a present or potential threat to human health, safety, welfare, or to the environment. Toxic or hazardous
  23| materials include any synthetic organic chemical, petroleum product, heavy metal, radioactive or infectious waste,
  24| acid and alkali, and any substance defined as toxic or hazardous under MGL c. 21C and c. 21E, and the regulations
  25| at 310 CMR 30.000 and 310 CMR 40.0000.
  26| WASTEWATER
  27| Any sanitary waste, sludge, or septic tank or cesspool overflow, and water that during manufacturing, cleaning or
  28| processing, comes into direct contact with or results from the production or use of any raw material, intermediate
  29| product, finished product, byproduct or waste product.
  30| WATERCOURSE
  31| A natural or man-made channel through which water flows or a stream of water, including a river, brook or
  32| underground stream.
  33| WATERS OF THE COMMONWEALTH
  34| All waters within the jurisdiction of the commonwealth, including, without limitation, rivers, streams, lakes, ponds,
  35| springs, impoundments, estuaries, wetlands, and groundwater.
  36| C. Applicability. This bylaw shall apply to flows entering the municipally owned storm sewerage system and waters of
  37| the commonwealth.
  38| D. Authority. This bylaw is adopted pursuant to the regulations of the Federal Clean Water Act found at 40 CFR 122.34
  39| and the Phase II ruling from the Environmental Protection Agency found in the December 8, 1999 Federal Register.
  40| E. Responsibility for administration. The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission,

===PAGE 183=== (plain, 36 lines)
   1| 183
   2| Department of Public Works Director and Building Commissioner, shall administer, implement and enforce this bylaw.
   3| Any powers granted to or duties imposed upon the above may be delegated in writing by the above to employees or
   4| agents of the above.
   5| F. Regulations. The Planning Board may promulgate rules and regulations to effectuate the purposes of this bylaw.
   6| Failure by the Planning Board to promulgate such rules and regulations shall not have the effect of suspending or
   7| invalidating this bylaw.
   8| G. Prohibited activities.
   9| (1) Illicit discharges. No person shall dump, discharge, cause or allow to be discharged any pollutant or non-stormwater
  10| discharge into the municipal separate storm sewer system (MS4), into a watercourse, or into the waters of the
  11| commonwealth.
  12| (2) Illicit connections. No person shall construct, use, allow, maintain or continue any illicit connection to the municipal
  13| storm sewer system, regardless of whether the connection was permissible under applicable law, regulation or
  14| custom at the time of connection.
  15| (3) Obstruction of municipal storm sewer system. No person shall obstruct or interfere with the normal flow of
  16| stormwater into or out of the municipal storm sewer system without prior written approval from the Planning Board.
  17| H. Exemptions:
  18| (1) Discharge or flow resulting from fire-fighting activities.
  19| (2) The following non-stormwater discharges or flows are exempt from the prohibition of non-stormwaters, provided
  20| that the source is not a significant contributor of a pollutant to the municipal storm sewer system:
  21| (a) Water line flushing;
  22| (b) Flow from potable water sources;
  23| (c) Springs;
  24| (d) Natural flow from riparian habitats and wetlands;
  25| (e) Diverted stream flow;
  26| (f) Rising groundwater;
  27| (g) Uncontaminated groundwater infiltration as defined in 40 CFR 35.2005(20), or uncontaminated pumped
  28| groundwater;
  29| (h) Water from exterior foundation drains, footing drains (not including active groundwater dewatering systems), crawl
  30| space pumps, or air conditioning condensation;
  31| (i) Discharge from landscape irrigation or lawn watering;
  32| (j) Water from individual residential car washing;
  33| (k) Discharge from dechlorinated swimming pool water (less than one ppm chlorine), provided the water is allowed to
  34| stand for one week prior to draining and the pool is drained in such a way as not to cause a nuisance;
  35| (l) Discharge from street sweeping;
  36| (m) Dye testing, provided verbal notification is given to the Planning Board prior to the time of the test;

===PAGE 184=== (plain, 44 lines)
   1| 184
   2| (n) Non-stormwater discharge permitted under an NPDES permit or a surface water discharge permit, waiver, or waste
   3| discharge order administered under the authority of the United States Environmental Protection Agency or the
   4| Department of Environmental Protection, provided that the discharge is in full compliance with the requirements of
   5| the permit, waiver, or order and applicable laws and regulations;
   6| (o) Discharge for which advanced written approval is received from the Planning Board as necessary to protect public
   7| health, safety, welfare or the environment.
   8| (3) Discharge or flow that results from exigent conditions and occurs during a state of emergency declared by any
   9| agency of the federal or state government, or by the Town of Lunenburg Town Manager, the Planning Board or the
  10| Board of Health.
  11| I. Emergency suspension of storm sewerage system access. The Planning Board may suspend municipal storm sewer
  12| system access to any person or property without prior written notice when such suspension is necessary to stop an
  13| actual or threatened discharge of pollutants that presents imminent risk of harm to the public health, safety, welfare
  14| or the environment. In the event any person fails to comply with an emergency suspension order, the authorized
  15| enforcement agency may take all reasonable steps to prevent or minimize harm to the public health, safety, welfare
  16| or the environment.
  17| J. Notification of spills. Notwithstanding other requirements of local, state or federal law, as soon as a person
  18| responsible for a facility or operation, or responsible for emergency response for a facility or operation has
  19| information of or suspects a release of materials at that facility or operation resulting in or which may result in
  20| discharge of pollutants to the municipal drainage system or waters of the commonwealth, the person shall take all
  21| necessary steps to ensure containment and cleanup of the release. In the event of a release of oil or hazardous
  22| materials, the person shall immediately notify the municipal fire and police departments and the Planning Board and
  23| the Lunenburg Board of Health. In the event of a release of nonhazardous material, the reporting person shall notify
  24| the Planning Board no later than the next business day. The reporting person shall provide to the Planning Board
  25| written confirmation of all telephone, facsimile or in-person notifications within three business days thereafter. If the
  26| discharge of prohibited materials is from a commercial or industrial facility, the facility owner or operator of the
  27| facility shall retain on-site a written record of the discharge and the actions taken to prevent its recurrence. Such
  28| records shall be retained for at least three years.
  29| K. Enforcement.
  30| (1) The Building Commissioner, in conjunction with the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW Director,
  31| shall enforce this bylaw, regulations, orders, violation notices, and enforcement orders, and may pursue all civil and
  32| criminal remedies for such violations.
  33| (2) Civil relief. If a person violates the provisions of this bylaw, regulations, permit, notice, or order issued thereunder,
  34| the Planning Board, through the Building Commissioner, may seek injunctive relief in a court of competent
  35| jurisdiction restraining the person from activities which would create further violations or compelling the person to
  36| perform abatement or remediation of the violation.
  37| (3) Orders.
  38| (a) The Planning Board, in conjunction with the Conservation Commission and DPW Director, may issue a written
  39| order to the Building Commissioner to enforce the provisions of this bylaw or the regulations thereunder, which may
  40| include:
  41| [1] Elimination of illicit connections or discharges to the MS4;
  42| [2] Performance of monitoring, analyses, and reporting;
  43| [3] That unlawful discharges, practices, or operations shall cease and desist; and
  44| [4] Remediation of contamination in connection therewith.

===PAGE 185=== (plain, 40 lines)
   1| 185
   2| (b) If the Building Commissioner, in conjunction with Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW Director,
   3| determines that abatement or remediation of contamination is required, the order shall set forth a deadline by which
   4| such abatement or remediation must be completed. Said order shall further advise that, should the violator or
   5| property owner fail to abate or perform remediation within the specified deadline, the Town may, at its option,
   6| undertake such work, and expenses thereof shall be charged to the violator.
   7| (4) Costs. Within 30 days after completing all measures necessary to abate the violation or to perform remediation, the
   8| violator and the property owner will be notified of the costs incurred by the Town, including administrative costs.
   9| The violator or property owner may file a written protest objecting to the amount or basis of costs with the Planning
  10| Board within 30 days of receipt of the notification of the costs incurred. If the amount due is not received by the
  11| expiration of the time in which to file a protest or within 30 days following a decision of the Planning Board
  12| affirming or reducing the costs, or from a final decision of a court of competent jurisdiction, the costs shall become a
  13| special assessment against the property owner and shall constitute a lien on the owner's property for the amount of
  14| said costs. Interest shall begin to accrue on any unpaid costs at the statutory rate provided in MGL c. 59, § 57 after
  15| the 31st day at which the costs first become due.
  16| (5) Noncriminal disposition. As an alternative to criminal prosecution or civil action, the Planning Board may elect,
  17| through the Building Commissioner, to utilize the noncriminal disposition procedure set forth in MGL c. 40, § 21D,
  18| in which case the Building Commissioner of the Town shall be the enforcing person. The penalty for the first
  19| violation shall be $25. The penalty for the second violation shall be $50. The penalty for the third violation shall be
  20| $100. The penalty for the fourth violation and subsequent violations shall be $200. Each day or part thereof that such
  21| violation occurs or continues shall constitute a separate offense.
  22| (6) Entry to perform duties. Under this bylaw, to the extent permitted by state law, or if authorized by the owner or other
  23| party in control of the property, the Planning Board, its agents, and employees may enter upon privately owned
  24| property for the purpose of performing their duties under this bylaw and regulations and may make or cause to be
  25| made such examinations, surveys or sampling as the Planning Board deems reasonably necessary.
  26| (7) Appeals. The decisions or orders of the Planning Board, Conservation Commission and DPW shall be final. Further
  27| relief shall be to a court of competent jurisdiction.
  28| (8) Remedies not exclusive. The remedies listed in this bylaw are not exclusive of any other remedies available under
  29| any applicable federal, state or local law.
  30| L. Severability. The provisions of this bylaw are hereby declared to be severable. If any provision, paragraph, sentence,
  31| or clause of this bylaw or the application thereof to any person, establishment, or circumstances shall be held invalid,
  32| such invalidity shall not affect the other provisions or application of this bylaw.
  33| M. Transitional provisions. Residential property owners shall have 180 days from the effective date of the bylaw to
  34| comply with its provisions or petition the Planning Board for an extension, provided good cause is shown for the
  35| failure to comply with the bylaw during the specified period. All other property owners shall have 180 days from the
  36| effective date of the bylaw to comply with its provisions or petition the Planning Board for an extension, provided
  37| good cause is shown for the failure to comply with the bylaw during the specified period. VOTED
  38| UNANIMOUSLY as printed in the warrant. (The only change from Zoning to General Bylaw is italicized in section
  39| C. Applicability)
  40| Meeting Adjourned at 10:02 P.M.
